#6713. Kim and Khloé Kardashian’s Beauty Trip Didn’t Quite Go to Plan—Here’s What to Know Before Booking Yours

Between the salmon sperm facials, the skin boosters, and the next generation of lasers, there’s plenty to pique the interest of the beauty obsessive in South Korea. So much so that vacations are increasingly being organized around visits to the dermatologist’s office. But how much is too much when it comes to our beauty itineraries? Just ask Khloé and Kim Kardashian.

On Wednesday’s episode of her podcast, Khloé in Wonder Land, Koko and her close friend La La Anthony reminisced about their August 2025 trip to South Korea, where they enthusiastically availed themselves of the country’s advanced cosmetic treatments—with some less-than-desirable results.

“Kim and I came back deformed, you came back beautiful and the plane ride just made it so much worse,” Kardashian told Anthony, recalling how their post-treatment appearances had kept them holed up in their hotel room.

The Kardashians are, of course, well-versed in the world of aesthetics. Over the years, the sisters have been particularly candid about their experiments with everything from the vampire facial to regenerative skin treatments. But even for those with the world’s most sought-after dermatologists on speed dial, things don’t always go to plan.

While it’s unclear precisely which procedures the sisters underwent—photos shared on Kim’s Instagram at the time showed her receiving facial treatments and an IV infusion—their experience raises some pertinent questions. How much research should we be doing before traveling abroad for cosmetic treatments? How many procedures can we feasibly—and safely—fit into a vacation? And how do we know when something has actually gone wrong?

The lure of a beauty vacation is understandable. South Korea, in particular, is at the forefront of aesthetic innovation, with technologies and treatments that aren’t yet widely available in the United States. And as our collective beauty literacy has expanded, so too has our appetite for more technologically sophisticated treatments. A facial no longer necessarily means an hour of extractions and massage. But there is a distinction to be made between a beauty treatment and a medical procedure. Just because something is marketed as minimally invasive, or requires no incisions, doesn’t mean it is without risk. Nor does its ubiquity on TikTok necessarily speak to its suitability for your own skin.

“Traveling for treatment isn't the same as having unsafe treatment,” says consultant dermatologist Dr. Sidra Khan. “What travel adds is complexity: a different regulatory system, possibly a language barrier, often a compressed consultation, and being thousands of miles from your practitioner during recovery.”

“A beautiful clinic, an impressive Instagram following and a gallery of celebrity clients don’t really tell you much about the practitioner’s medical credentials or what results you might expect,” adds cosmetic surgeon and aesthetics specialist Dr. Paul Banwell. Independently verify your practitioner’s qualifications and registration with the relevant medical regulator, and establish whether the clinic is equipped to manage complications. Khan recommends arranging a virtual consultation with the treating doctor before booking anything, and asking about their experience with your particular skin tone, the products and devices they intend to use, and why. “A holiday itinerary shouldn't replace informed consent.”

For London-based aesthetics and NHS doctor Dr. Alicia Gonzalez-Fernandez, the marketing of medical procedures as vacation packages is itself a red flag. “On a handful of occasions, I have encountered patients that have come into A&E straight from the airport,” she says of her experiences working in emergency medicine. She cautions against practitioners who pressure patients into additional procedures or fail to adequately discuss complications and aftercare. “We are not in the business of making people self-conscious about things that are not a problem for them.”

Then there’s the phenomenon of “treatment stacking.” While certain procedures can complement one another, squeezing several into a short period of time can increase the likelihood of complications. “Many of these treatments work by creating a controlled injury that the skin then repairs, and doing several close together doesn't give it time to recover,” explains aesthetics doctor Dr. Priya Verma. “That raises the risk of prolonged inflammation, infection and pigmentation changes, particularly in darker skin tones.”

Multiple procedures can also make it harder to establish which treatment is responsible for an adverse reaction, notes aesthetics doctor Dr. Ahmed El Muntasar. “Ultimately, I’d much rather a patient’s treatment schedule was dictated by how their skin is recovering than by the date of their flight home.”

Consider, too, how conducive the rest of your vacation itinerary is to your skin’s recovery. As Verma notes, the low humidity in airplane cabins can exacerbate dryness and sensitivity in already compromised skin, while sun exposure can increase the risk of post-inflammatory hyperpigmentation. There is no universal rule about how soon you can fly after a non-surgical cosmetic procedure, says Khan, but you should allow ample time for recovery and an in-person review before your departure. (With surgical procedures, flying also carries an increased risk of blood clots, making a conversation with your surgeon about your travel plans particularly important.)

There's also the question of downtime. An unfortunate-looking reflection in the hotel bathroom mirror doesn’t necessarily mean a treatment has been “botched.” Verma makes an important distinction: “Downtime is the expected redness, swelling or flaking that settles over a few days. Dissatisfaction is when the skin has healed but you're not happy with the result. A complication is a medical problem that needs treating.”

Knowing what to expect—and for how long—is something to establish with your practitioner well before the treatment itself. The trajectory of your recovery is key: Increasing pain, spreading redness, heat, discharge, or fever warrant prompt medical attention.

There are, however, symptoms that warrant immediate medical attention. Following dermal filler injections, for example, the FDA advises seeking immediate medical care for unusual pain, changes to your vision, white, gray, or blue discoloration around the injection site, or signs of a stroke. These may indicate that the blood supply has been compromised.

“If there are red-flag symptoms, don't just get on a plane because you'd rather deal with it at home,” stresses Khan. “Some complications are time-critical, so get seen locally.”

In non-emergency situations, contact your original practitioner for an in-person review and document your recovery with photographs taken in consistent lighting. El Muntasar recommends obtaining your full treatment records, including the names, quantities, and batch numbers of any injectables, or the device and settings used for laser treatments. Seek an independent medical opinion from someone experienced in managing complications if you remain concerned.

As distressing as it can be to feel unhappy with your appearance, resist the temptation to immediately book another aesthetic procedure. “Swelling can make certain areas appear overfilled or disproportionate, and adding more filler to try to balance things out can leave a patient with an entirely different problem once that swelling subsides,” explains Banwell.

“More treatment isn’t always the antidote to treatment,” says Khan. Anti-wrinkle injections can take around two weeks to reach their full effect, filler can take several weeks to settle, and collagen remodeling following laser treatments or microneedling continues for months. The same caution applies to overzealous at-home skincare. “Your freshly lasered face is not the moment to try the acids and retinoids you picked up at duty-free.”

And while you might have meticulously mapped out your Seoul shopping itinerary, restaurant reservations, and dermatology appointments, have you given quite as much thought to what happens when you get home? Establishing a plan for follow-up care, obtaining a record of your procedures, and checking whether your insurance covers complications should all factor into the planning process. El Muntasar recommends getting your aftercare plan in writing, including details of who you can contact outside of clinic hours and who is responsible for any additional medical expenses should you need to extend your stay.

By all means, indulge your K-beauty curiosity. Just make sure you’re approaching your dermatological appointments with a little more discernment than your haul at Olive Young.

I’m talking cocktail and wedding guest dresses, knit styles, and even sporty little numbers you can wear to the gym—all under $50.

Suppose you’re ever in need of a dress for an upcoming event or just because (fair enough), keep scrolling. There’s something for every personal style and budget, promise!

Nice, right? There are even more options below! And while we're here, can I interest ya in some shoes to complete the look? We've got ballet flats (balletcore is thriving, peeps), knee-high boots, and (just generally) the very best boots on Amazon. Just save those tabs for now as you take a deep dive into these cutie dresses. Now, shop!

This maxi-length knit number is one of those throw-on-and-go dresses that will make you feel instantly put-together. You can style it up or down, pile on the accessories or keep it simple, and look like you put considerably more effort into your ‘fit than you actually did.

If you’re always freezing at the function, this poncho sweater dress will be your saving grace. You won’t have to buy a separate layer just to survive the aggressively low thermostat setting. This style literally comes with one!

Lean into your preppy alter ego with this short-sleeve polo dress that looks so chic with a pair of loafers or boat shoes. It’s giving country club in the best way, especially paired with a little shoulder bag and sleek sunnies.

It doesn’t get much more classic than this boatneck mini dress, which was practically made for a cocktail attire occasion. The A-line silhouette is universally flattering, and the simple yet elegant design makes it so easy to reinvent no one will remember you wore it to the last wedding!

Allow me to introduce you to your new favorite dress. Instead of the classic wrap style you probably already have hanging in your closet, try this tie-waist design that creates the same cinching effect—only it’s even more flattering.

When it comes to shopping for a wedding guest dress, you want to prioritize versatility so it isn’t just a one-time wear. Take this $40 Amazon dress, for example. The classic, strapless design (which includes a silicone grip to actually stay in place!) works for so many different dress codes. Plus, you can accessorize it differently each time to avoid the outfit-repeater award.

This midi wrap dress is the type of style you can throw on and feel instantly put-together—without spending 30-minutes agonizing over your closet. It’s made with a soft, stretchy ribbed knit material and has a tie-waist belt that cinches at the most flattering part of your waist.

A PSA to all my brides: If you don’t want to spend your entire wardrobe budget on the shower dress, Amazon’s got your back. I mean, hello, look at this darling champagne style with short ruffled sleeves and luxe satin fabric. The bottom line? It’s so timeless and romantic, you’ll wanna rewear it for more occasions.

Hit the tennis (or pickleball) court with in this style, or wear it to run errands. And then put five more in your cart because you're about to love it so much, you’ll want one in every color.

Who doesn’t love a dress with a little movement, especially if you’re planning to wear it to a wedding where you’ll spend the night on the dance floor. This ruffle maxi understands the assignment.

This bestselling polka-dot style—which comes in a ton of other patterns too, including florals—will do all the talking for your outfit. Pair it with a strappy sandal and shoulder bag for the warmer months, or transition it for winter with pointed-toe boots and cropped jacket on top.

In case you missed the memo: a dress can be just as comfortable as your favorite sweats, and this maxi-length design is proof. It’s the kind of style you’ll reach for on lazy Sundays and dress-up for evenings out.

A major perk about this boho maxi? You can adjust the tie straps so that it fits comfortably, all day long. Plus, some colors (and patterns!) are currently on sale.

This is the type of slip dress that you can wear for almost any occasion. Pair it with ballet flats and a wrap sweater for a more casual approach, or dress it up with heels or a blazer to make it work appropriate.

If you’re not living in sweater dresses once the temps hit sub-40, you’re doing it wrong. They’re ridiculously comfortable and so easy to style, you’ll avoid the routine “I have nothing to wear” meltdown.

#6708. 11 Engagement Nail Ideas That Feel Romantic, Polished, and Photo-Ready

From milky whites to glitter tips, these manicures are made for ring photos.

Engagement season is officially upon us (I should know; I got engaged in December!), and with it comes a very particular kind of pressure: the perfect manicure for the perfect moment. Because once the ring is on, your hands become the main event, shared in photos, texted to loved ones, and passed around group chats.

But a great engagement manicure is about more than looking good on camera. At the end of the day, it comes down to preference and the way a set of nails makes you feel. Some brides-to-be find their calm in a sheer, milky pink; others feel most like themselves in a soft almond shape or a crisp, modern French tip. A glossy top coat might be your idea of perfection, while a hint of shimmer feels more you. There’s no wrong answer here. The best engagement nails are the ones that make you want to hold your hand out and admire it, because when you feel polished and put together, it shows in every photo, and in every happy-tear hug that follows.

Ahead, some ideas for the best engagement nails to wear while you celebrate, from the soft, understated shades that let your ring do the talking to the bolder finishes that show off your personality.

Polished and understated, this pearly white manicure is pure timeless elegance, with a subtle glow that sets the stage for the moment you say yes.

This toasted cappuccino mani brings warmth and richness to your engagement look, a creamy, neutral brown that flatters every skin tone and glows like the diamond against it.

A milky white manicure is anything but boring. It’s soft, sheer, and quietly luminous, with a clean, polished finish that makes your hands look effortlessly put together.

Delicate yet eye-catching, this manicure catches the light in all the right ways, making it a romantic choice that complements your ring without ever competing with it.

A sparkly manicure doubles as festive and romantic, making it an ideal pick for an engagement during the holiday season. With its glittering finish catching the light from every angle, it’s a celebration in itself.

Don’t be afraid to go with color! (I was wearing a dark espresso when I got engaged, and I wouldn’t change a thing.) A moody mauve is proof that a deeper shade can feel just as romantic as a soft neutral. Sophisticated and a little unexpected, it’s an excellent pick for anyone who wants their engagement manicure to feel unmistakably them.

A deep cocoa cat-eye with textured dewdrops gives depth and personality to your engagement look, with a rich chocolate base that shimmers and shifts as your hand catches the light. The glossy droplets add a playful, dimensional touch, perfect for anyone who wants something a little different that still feels romantic and perfectly suited to a winter celebration.

Nearly bare nails featuring a tiny French manicure are proof that less really is more. With a sheer, natural-looking base and a delicate whisper of white along the tip, this look feels fresh, minimal, and effortlessly refined.

Another shade that plays into the holiday season, this metallic silver manicure brings a cool, icy shimmer to your engagement look. Sleek and glamorous, it pairs beautifully with platinum and white gold bands and makes a diamond sparkle even brighter.

Subtle yet equally stunning, this glazed ombré blends a light beige base into soft brown tips for a look that feels warm, modern, and beautifully blended. It’s a lovely pick for anyone who wants a neutral manicure with a little extra depth, and it lets your ring shine while still feeling special every time you glance down.

So glam, so chic, this silvery white nail set brings a frosty, high-shine finish to your engagement look, with a metallic gleam. Polished and dazzling, it’s a standout choice for anyone who wants their manicure to feel luxe and celebratory.

#6707. 17 Best Beard Trimmer Picks, Tested by GQ Editors in 2026

Every guy needs the best beard trimmer for his situation. Whether he grows it patchy, keeps it perma-stubbled, or is as lustrous as the A-List beards, a trimmer is mandatory in every beard kit. And not all beard trimmers are alike, which is why the best one for your facial hair style may be different from the next guy’s.

I tested a bunch of beard trimmers to deliver you a real who’s who of men’s grooming devices. This list reflects a range of options—some multitaskers, some detailers; some top-shelf, some a better buy for lower budgets. Above all, though, I ask you this: Make sure that the device you choose allows your beard to reach its full potential. That doesn’t mean you need a big bushy beard, but rather, you should be able to achieve whichever style (or a suite of styles) you want to wear over the next few years—which is the minimum amount of time any of these devices should last. Ideally, they’ll hum on for five to 10 years, anyhow.

Here are the best beard trimmers after we tested pretty much every last one of them. (And while you’re at it, consider my rosters of other grooming tools, from razors and electric shavers, to manscapers and hair clippers.)

Black Friday is here, and the deals are hitting fast. For the biggest and swerviest markdowns, editor favorites, and elite-level shopping intel, keep it locked on our roundup of the best Black Friday deals and best Black Friday clothing deals.

No sense in burying the headline: Philips Norelco’s Multigroom 9000 Series is, hands down, the best beard trimmer you can buy. And that’s before factoring in how much more it can do than just keep your facial hair in check than other electric razors that are found in your everyday grooming kit. (It’s also recently been renamed the “All-in-One 9000” but answers to both names.) For the last 5 or 6 years, this has been my single go-to trimmer. Everything else is trying to dethrone it, and so far, no true contest has emerged. In terms of customization and power, this device checks all of the boxes.

It all starts with the beating heart: "The Multigroom does not compromise on power or battery life, and it's very intuitive to use," says Fellow Barber’s Head of Education Emily Clark. The battery life truly over-delivers: your mileage may vary since Philips Norelco only claims a (still impressive) six hours of charge, but I got a ridiculous 17 hours of consecutive runtime fresh out of the box, thanks to a lithium ion battery. Speaking of the box, there’s a lot in there, all of it useful. Between the small tooth-like detail attachment and foil shaving head, the Multigroom 9000 makes it easy to style and sculpt with precision. One trimmer even adjusts by tenths of a millimeter to help you get the perfect designer stubble, if that's your aim. And that’s just its beard trimming bona fides. The Multigroom 9000 is also a solid hair trimmer (shout out to that brawny motor and battery again) and body hair trimmer as well. Covering off for three products, and doing it well, suddenly makes the $90 price tag look like a deal.

Long-term testing notes: Wouldn’t you know it… I lost the beloved little shaver on this one recently. Heartbreak. (Yeah, I can test a new one, but I realize now how much of my love for it is pinned on that micro shaving attachment.) I stand by the fact that it’s the best on the market, but without that shaver, the window has opened for me to spend time with other devices, like the Braun below. Let this be a lesson: Don’t unpack your Philips Norelco all-in-one in a hotel room without carefully auditing every single piece when you pack it up again. Adam Hurly

"This trimmer is great! It comes with so many attachments to use for your trimming needs. The battery lasts a long time, i have yet to have to charge mine and i've had it for a few months now.

The trimmer is super quiet as well as very well made. It's all made up of metal, not plastic, and feels like it will last a long time. It's fairly lightweight and easy to handle when [using] it. It also comes with a small cleaning brush that makes cleaning it up a breeze!"

Given how much I love the Philips Norelco Multigroom 9000, it’s no surprise that the more affordable model, the Multigroom 5000, is my budget choice to keep things manscaped. It offers most of the same steel-bladed precision and customization benefits as its fancier family member, albeit in a less durable body (plastic instead of stainless steel) and half the battery life. Still, its a three-hour battery power beast that bests many beard trimmers twice the price. And the lighter plastic body is nice for travel. The Philips Norelco Multigroom 5000 is great for anyone on a budget, but it’s not “budget."

Whereas other lower-priced beard trimmers can’t handle precise detailing, the Multigroom 5000 includes a detail trimmer and nose trimmer, not to mention hair clipper and body trimmer guards for versatility. It does lack the shaving detailer and the body shaver (as does the in-between 7000 model), which is important to note if you want that level of customization and don’t otherwise own a detail shaver that you also rely upon for bare-faced stretches of facial real estate. For this reason, I find it a little less perfect across the board compared to the 9000, but the price-to-precision ratio is hard to match. And if you want a great value above all—and a straightforward beard trimmer—meet your optimal Multigroom, the 5000.

Long-term testing notes: Recently, I used the 5000 consistently, and got quite cozy with it. Sure, it lacked the key shaving features of the 9000, but I think this is truly the best value device in the game when you only want a trimmer. Terrific muscle, terrific customization, great endurance. Five stars for this one. Adam Hurly

"This Phillips/Norelco trimmer works great. It is very quiet, the battery life is pretty long and it does exactly what I need it to do. It comes with a lot of attachments - haven’t used them all, but the ones I have used worked just fine. This is a good quality trimmer."

Hatteker is a surprise of a buy—it’s that “out of left field” Amazon brand that most of us recognize by name, despite not knowing anyone who uses it. This all-in-one kit covers every kind of hair-trimming task, from lineups to sideburns to beards, eyebrows, and body hair. What's more, it's a hair-cutting machine for full beards and stubble alike with multiple plastic guards. I bought the tool with little expectations for quality and care, but was consistently surprised at how well it held its own compared to the more reputable brands.

This device won’t win any design awards and certainly hasn’t reinvented the wheel, but most consumers won’t care about that stuff; they want a good beard trimmer that meets all their grooming needs, and Hatteker’s checks all of those boxes. I do wish its detailing trimmer was a bit narrower, but that’s me minding some very fine details. It’s also not up to the same quality as, say, Philips, Andis, Braun, Wahl, etc, but it’s also not low quality either. For $40, you get your money’s worth, especially considering that LED battery display and vast range of trimming lengths and clipping attachments. It’s still a great option if you’re not looking to spend a lot, or if our other best value winner, the Philips Norelco Multigroom 5000 Series, is out of stock.

Long-term testing notes: Every time I bring this one out, I am amused by how surprisingly great it is. It goes to show that those Amazon reviews really are one of the most accurate depictions of value; I long wrote the brand off because of its non-image compared to the big names, but in terms of performance and return on spend, Hatteker really clears the bar. Granted, I’m not using most of these devices more than a few times a quarter (since by and large I stick with my own favorites or test new ones) but my Hatteker remains strong and sturdy and has endurance, some three years after I first acquired it. Adam Hurly

"The trimmer is easy to use and the charge lasts a very long time. I have not had to recharge since the initial charge after taking it out of the box, trimming bi-weekly. It is not heavy. It’s easy to handle. The adjustment guides stay where you select. I don’t worry about them shifting during use. Beard maintenance is an easy job with this.”

The All-in-One 7000 makes a convincing case for clearing some space in your bathroom cabinet. A body hair trimmer that comes replete with several beard-related attachments, its precision comb kept stubble neatly between 1-3mm without pulling or leaving stray hairs behind. Sure, a dedicated beard trimmer will do a more precise job, but there's plenty of substance to this device's delirious accessory count.

The slightly boxy handle feels reassuringly secure when I'm cleaving away at a couple of days' growth, while a textured grip makes it well-suited to comfortably swerving across my chest, underarms, and more sensitive areas. If you dig them out from the bottom of their box, the nose and ear attachments prove useful enough to earn their place in the kit too. Philips rates this kit for up to five hours between charges – handy given how often you'll be using the thing – and the waterproof construction makes cleanup straightforward.

My main gripe is one that always crops up when I test Philips, Braun, and other established grooming brands’ devices – the proprietary charging connection. You’ll need its supplied cable, even if your drawer is already overflowing with USB-C leads. Still, for someone who wants to maintain short facial hair and keep the rest of their grooming in order with one tool, the 7000 covers a lot of ground. Esat Dedezade

“It's comfortable and quiet with an easy-to-adjust head that's great for both beard and mustache maintenance. The precision comb is handy for controlling length too, and there's very little tugging around the neck. And if you leave it a little too long to get your beard into shape? It holds its charge surprisingly well when tucked away in a bathroom cabinet.”

Like the Philips Norelco Multigroom models, Braun's All-in-One Trimmer Series 9 is a jack-of-all-trades option that can do far more than tidy up your beard; the box is packed with a nose and ear trimmer, a precision head, and longer guards, too. But what separates this trimmer from the rest of the pack is the built-in dial that offers 40 trim-length settings. It’s top-notch precision in minimalist packaging. I think it’s the only real contender for the top spot on this list—perhaps that price and slightly less enduring battery are its shortcomings compared to the Philips Norelco 9000, but I do find it a superior device in terms of ease and design.

Where other devices require you to swap out different heads or attachments, or ask you to pick between bigger length gaps than you’d prefer, Braun’s Series 9 offers half-millimeter increments from 0.5 to 20 without having to futz with extra accessories. In my testing, that made the trimmer the most user-friendly device on this list—no swapping guards as you fade your beard or neckline. Plus, the Series 9 is fully waterproof for those of you shaving-in-the-shower fans looking to speedrun your morning regimen. (And I’ll admit, shaving in the shower certainly makes clean-up—of both the beard hair mess and the trimmer—faster and easier.) Bottom line, Braun’s All-in-One Trimmer Series 9 is exactly what the name promises. If it seems a little expensive, that’s because a beard trimmer that can handle all these tasks, and handle them well, isn’t cheap.

Long-term testing notes: As I said above, this one has been getting lots more mileage from me lately, and I am so pleased with that fact. I absolutely adore how easy it is to navigate between the smallest minutiae of trimming lengths without swapping guard heads, and that detail shaver has been so clutch at keeping my neckline, cheeks, and beard perimeter cleaned up. My one complaint from a recent trip was that it’s quite difficult to repack everything into the carrying case, if you bring along all attachment heads. Besides that… perfect grooming tool. Adam Hurly

“Before this trimmer came into my life, I made a couple of cheap purchases. The style I like it's 1mm overall with a 2mm on my chin and 2.5 on my mustache. The problem was the other trimmers could not handle it on a daily basis. I am the type of guy whose beard grows back really fast, so I'd trim my beard. Then, the next day, you could see clear growth, and when I tried to do it again, it just wouldn't trim the same. I'd end up hurting my face a little bit. Not this trimmer. This trimmer can handle my beard on a daily basis without hurting my face. It's that good and efficient. It's built for heavy performance and durability. For the first time I am able to maintain my beard on a daily basis.”

Wahl is a pedigreed name in the professional and home grooming worlds, but that doesn’t mean it’s done innovating. Its new High Visibility trimmer has a killer app: the trimming attachment is angled so the teeth sit “above” the base, letting you get eyes on the action in the mirror as you mow through facial hair. It’s maybe not a priority if you’re just doing leisurely trims, but if you’re trying to get the perfect line at your cheek, sideburns, or neck, that little bit of offset is a godsend.

Suddenly I’m seeing very clearly exactly where the blade is hitting. If you’ve ever had to drop that cheek line too far down or do the back-and-forth thing with your burns, you’ll recognize what a game changer this is. The Wahl High Visibility trimmer’s laser-pointer precision is boosted by some of the best ergonomics in the game. The body’s relatively thin with an excellent rubber grip, which lets you hit difficult angles like a professional pool player. The High Visibility trimmer is new to the market, but I’m betting it’ll become a barber favorite—and yours, too, if you’re doing daily touch-ups to keep those lines looking sharp.

Long-term testing notes: I find myself using this more often as a detail trimmer than a full-on trimmer. I think it’s a fantastic trimmer through and through, but I pull it from the bullpen as a closer; that’s where I hold it mostly in my mind—and happily! Adam Hurly

"Absolutely amazing product. Heavy, rock-solid construction makes the hand feel confident, has great ergonomics, and has a solid grip even when the hand is soapy. But the best part is the forever blade makes it easy to see what’s getting cut, even with guard on (I used the 2). Most precise edge for a jawbone line beard I’ve ever had. And with the guard, it combs as it cuts with the grain of the hair, keeping stray whiskers knocked down. I’ve been pinched/cut before with fatter spaced blade rakes, but I didn’t feel threatened trimming under the nose."

The MultiShape lets you build a grooming kit around the things you actually use. Start with this beard and hair trimmer, then add a nose trimmer, foil shaver, or even a toothbrush head if you want them. Everything shares one rechargeable handle, which makes much more sense than finding room for another charger every time you buy a new device. Other manufacturers – take note.

This starter kit includes the lithium-ion base, beard and hair head, and two combs. The other attachments cost extra, so factor those in before deciding how much money or luggage space it’ll save. Panasonic rates the battery for 90 minutes, with a one-hour charge.

Long-term testing notes: Over several weeks, I used a broader MultiShape kit for shaving, beard maintenance, and brushing his teeth. Swapping heads was quick, and the light handle made the shared system easy to live with. The beard head coped with ordinary growth, although particularly stubborn hair exposed its limits. With daily toothbrushing and shaving three or four times a week, he charged the handle roughly every fortnight. That convenience made it a favorite for travel, while a dedicated beard trimmer remained the stronger choice for dense growth. Owen Gough

"The upside of buying a beard trimmer that can double-up as an electric toothbrush with the right attachment is you've got one tool to rule them all. The challenge? You might need a couple of passes with the trimming head to get an even finish. Compared with the more traditional alternative, it's a bulkier device with its guard fitted.”

For anyone whose morning routine involves keeping a few days’ growth under control, the dedicated Beard Trimmer 7000 keeps things pleasingly straightforward. Its adjustment dial covers 40 lengths, while the textured handle gives you something secure to hold before the first coffee has kicked in.

The dial is the main reason to choose this over a kit with a bag of fixed guards. You can adjust the length without hunting for the next comb, then use the detailing attachment to tidy the edges. It’s a more focused choice than Philips’ all-in-one kits if beard maintenance is the job you actually need done. Just don’t mistake the hair collector for a return of the old vacuum trimmer – it’s another part to empty and clean.

Long-term testing notes: I found the Series 7000 easygoing around my chin and sideburns, with little cleanup needed for stray hairs. Its reassuring weight and textured grip also helps, even if the plastic finish isn't quite on par with Philips' now-retired all-metal Series 9000. Truthfully, this trimmer did struggle a little around the awkward patch beneath my nostrils with its bulky combs making it harder to get a neat finish. I also don't bother much with the removable hair collector – I'd rather clean the sink once rather than wash another attachment. As an everyday trimmer, this is still a solid pick. Rob Leedham

“This trimmer is perfectly functional for its price, and its two-hour battery life means I'm yet to be caught short without any charge mid-shave. As much as I appreciate its efficiency, I do miss how the older metal design looked on my bathroom sink-top. This plastic iteration feels cheap (and a little flimsy) in comparison.”

No attachments; no problem. For a beard trimmer that can only do one thing (but is damn good at doing that), Manscaped's Beard Hedger will make light work out of the bushiest, burliest beards. Its zoom wheel controller lets you dial in the comb's length between .5 and 10 millimeters for even shaves, while the waterproof construction means you can shower and shave to save time on your morning or nighttime rituals. The built-in comb is great for picking up hairs that are stuck to your face so that every strand gets whacked, because few things are more unsightly than stray long hairs poking out from an otherwise well-groomed beard. Adam Hurly

“I've owned maybe 5 or 6 beard trimmers over the last 15 years. Make no mistake, this is the most expensive, but hands down it's also the best (that includes competition from Braun, Philips Norelco and Wahl). The quality is the first thing you notice as soon as you unbox it - the feel of the handle and the quality of the blade, even down to the USB charging cable which doesn't have the usual cheapness you'd expect from an accessory that's something of an afterthought.”

If you’re researching beard trimmers, you’ll see the term “zero-gap” a lot. So, what’s a zero-gap trimmer? A zero-gap trimmer cuts as close to the skin as possible, effectively a 0mm cutting length. That can make them dangerous for novices, as you can easily cut your skin or ears while you trim. But for the highest possible level of precision, that’s what you want. (The Bevel Trimmer can also be adjusted so it has a slightly longer taper.)

This trimmer was designed by Black men, for Black men, and damn does it look good. It’s a work of art that design lovers will appreciate. This sculptural device will look mighty impressive on your bathroom counter and feels phenomenal in your hand. It’s a true barber-quality trimmer, perfect for guys with curly or Afro-textured hair. It’s also one of the most powerful trimmers for home use, with a 6,000 RPM motor. Despite that, it beats all comers in terms of battery life, with an 8-hour charge that will last for months. A word of warning: This trimmer doesn’t come with any attachments or comb guards. That’s by design, as it’s a precision trimmer for making clean lines and shaping up longer beards. If you want to cut precise hair lengths, opt for the Bevel Trimmer Pro or purchase extra attachments like the T-Blade. Adam Hurly

"I’ve been cutting my own hair for years. Let me tell you these are the best trimmers I’ve ever used. Nothing on the market compares. I mean every word. The best!!!! Switch the original blade that comes with it out with the t-blade and I promise you that line up gone be crispy like bacon! Bevel with T-Blade is King!!!"

One more Philips Norelco device, for good measure and good reason: This tool is adored by both our expert source and me: I love the OneBlade for its ability to switch between trimmer, shaver, T-blade, and body groomer with aplomb. I think it's one of the best-designed tools in facial hair care right now, and a dozen other brands are releasing dupes to chase the same high.

“I appreciate how affordable it is and how easy it is to find at drugstores since so many clients ask me where to buy it because they love it too,” says DeZarate. “There was one week where I needed to buy three of the OneBlade for different clients during press events, so that they were able to actually travel home with it because they loved how easy it is to use and that it's universal. It does a really nice job at creating clean lines and is great for sensitive skin.” This one is easy to torque in different directions, too, allowing its shaving function to act like a T-blade trimmer while also offering familiar cartridge-shave movement for anyone who wants an entirely clean mug or simple a neckline tidy up. Get one with the body grooming attachment, too, and you could have an all-in-one grooming device for all facial and manscaping needs; it really does it all with that one singular blade.

Long-term testing notes: Like DeZarate, I frequently reach for this guy to mind the details; it’s an easy tag-along for any trips (especially if I need some body trimming all the while) and will certainly be with me on a big three-month trip I have to take soon; it’s a terrific head-to-toe customizer, though I don’t necessarily rely on it when I’m at home and have dedicated devices for each task. It is a terrific middle-ground pick, for the guy who wants things streamlined. Adam Hurly

“I have to admit, I’ve avoided this product for the past few years. For some reason I didn’t think it was something I needed. I recently started maintaining my beard on my own more and needed something with a bit more precision. Safety razors and straight razors were cutting up my face. My $200 electric razor cuts good, but is terrible at getting straight edges. So I figured I would give this a try and I must say I’m very happy with it. This is a very high quality product for the price. Not only does this razor provide the precision I needed to line up my beard, but it also shaves very very well.”

Vacuum trimmers keep you from having dozens of errant clippings littering the sink, but this one is more than just a vacuum—it has a detailing and edgework attachment and can trim up to 18 mm with a line-drawing detail head that rivals most T-blade trimmers.

Panasonic's wet/dry beard trimmer has a convenient wheel that lets you switch between guard lengths effortlessly—a huge boon when you're trying to shape your beard.

A display screen on your beard trimmer? In this economy? If it seems too good to be true, we get it, but this easy-adjusting trimmer from Panasonic is, in fact, the real deal.

For just 25 bucks, you can get this all-in-one trimmer from Philips that does a surprisingly good job when it comes to shaping and maintaining your scruff. Is it the fanciest trimmer on the list? No, but it works.

You want a beard trimmer that’s simple, effective, and doesn’t come with useless features or add-ons. Here are a few features to consider when looking for a great beard trimmer.

We look at how evenly a trimmer cuts, how comfortable it is to use, and whether its attachments make different jobs easier. Power matters, but so do the small frustrations that become obvious with regular use – awkward combs, difficult cleanup, or a handle that’s hard to hold. We also compare battery life, charging, and the range of cutting lengths against the price.

Adam Hurly has reviewed grooming products since 2013, and his years of testing underpin this guide. Timothy Beck Werth contributes his own hands-on findings alongside his wider coverage of grooming and consumer technology. For this update, we’ve also drawn on my reviews for British GQ, with the relevant tester and any differences between British and US kits identified in the entries. Read more about GQ and how we test and review products.

There’s no denying: The Philips Norelco Multigroom 9000 is the best beard trimmer you can buy these days. If its $100 price tag is too steep, the baby-brother Multigroom 5000 is a solid fallback. It’s not shower-proof, has a shorter warranty (five years to the 9000’s eight years), has a plastic body instead of steel, and comes with a few less attachments. But at $40, it gets the basics done nearly as well as it’s pricier sibling.

Can you use a beard trimmer to cut head hair?

Beard trimmers are not meant to be used as hair clippers, since clippers require much more power to cut through thicker patches of hair. However, some beard trimmers have the muscle to multitask, and they’ll proudly hype that skill—usually by declaring the work for “beard and head” or similar language. We usually verify those claims against product reviews, too. And frankly, we’d probably just opt to buy a dedicated hair trimmer (you can find our roundup of the best hair trimmers here), especially if you’ll be using it often.

Barbers choose tools to suit the job, from adjustable trimmers for controlling length, to detail trimmers for sharper edges. The Wahl Pro Series High Visibility is our professional pick for precision work, while the Philips Norelco Multigroom range offers broader versatility. For home use, start with your beard length and the kind of maintenance you do most often – a tool built for crisp outlines won’t necessarily include the guards you need to trim a longer beard evenly.

Which trimmer number is best for a beard?

That's going to depend on what beard style you're going with. In order to play around with trimmer numbers, we like the Braun All-in-One Trimmer Series 9 9440 for its easy-to-use adjustment wheel.

First, wash and dry your beard so you're starting off with a clean canvas. Then, decide how short or long you want to keep that bad boy. Trim against the grain of your growth, and know that the direction of growth may vary even in similar locations on the face. It's OK to make a couple of passes to ensure you get each hair because a patchy beard is a bad beard. Finally, clean up the neckline with some sharp lines so it's neat and tidy, framing the perfection that is your beautiful facial hair.

How do I maintain and clean a beard trimmer?

Switch it off and disconnect the charger before cleaning, then remove trapped hair from the blades and combs after each use. Follow the instructions for your particular model – a washable cutting head doesn’t mean the whole handle can go under the tap. The Remington Vacuum trimmer, for example, has removable washable parts, but its body should stay out of water.

Let washed components dry before putting them away. Oil the blades if the manufacturer tells you to, but don’t assume every trimmer needs it. Philips’ current Multigroom blades are designed to work without oiling. Replace a damaged or persistently blunt cutting unit with the correct part for your model.

How do I choose the right trimmer if I have sensitive skin or ingrown hairs?

Avoid “zero-gap” trimmers, which is a trimmer that cuts as close to the skin as possible, effectively a 0mm cutting length—those can mess your skin if you're prone to irritation or ingrowns. Otherwise, since you're trimming, you usually won't have to worry about irritating your skin because the blades shouldn't ever actually make contact with your face.

What’s the best way to travel with a beard trimmer—any TSA or voltage concerns?

One of the big concerns for frequent travelers—at least in the USA—is whether they're going to be able to get their beard trimmer through security and not having it buzz during transport. If this is a priority for you, look for a model with a travel lock like the Braun All-in-One Trimmer Series 9 9440, which prevents the trimmer from turning on in transit.

How do I clean a waterproof vs. non-waterproof trimmer?

Waterproof trimmers are a breeze: Just hit ‘em with the (usually included) cleaning brush, then wash them off in the sink before drying and applying some oil. Non-waterproof trimmers just skip the rinse, so you’ll have to take extra care with the brush. You can also use spray cleaners specifically designed for grooming electricals depending on your trimmer's care instructions.

What should I do if my trimmer starts pulling hair or losing power?

First, make sure you've thoroughly cleaned the trimmer. Next, make sure it's fully charged and/or has new batteries. If it's still pulling hair, it's probably time to swap the blades (plenty of companies sell refills on their site, and make sure to check the warranty to see if you qualify for a fresh set). If it's losing power, it might be time to swap the internal battery (check your warranty!) or get a new trimmer altogether. Luckily, we haven't had that issue with any of the trimmers listed above.

Melissa DeZarate is a celebrity groomer whose clients include Matt Damon, Kenan Thompson, Matt Bomer, and Taika Waititi.

#6701. The Most Flattering Hairstyles for Women Over 50, From Bobs to Long Layers

The most flattering styles are less about age and more about shape, movement, and hair health.

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

The rules are relative when it comes to “age-appropriate hair.” No matter what decade you’re presently thriving in, the style you choose should make you feel good. That said, as hair texture, volume, and color change with age, some styles may feel and look better once you hit your 50s. The options are vast, but don’t think you have to go shorter as you mature or cover up sprouting grays. If anything, feel free to experiment. Worn short hair all your life? Try some extensions and waist-length waves. Or if you’ve had long hair for as long as you can remember, trim a few inches off into a happy medium lob. Experts share tips and ideas for choosing the best hairstyle if you’re over 50.

The most important thing is to choose for the hair you have now. “I tell my clients to think about things like fullness, movement, tone, and shape,” Elba Iris, John Frieda stylist and educator, tells ELLE. A good style, Elba adds, should make hair look healthy and as full as possible, have movement so it never looks stiff or set, and have shine with dimension. It’s also helpful to be realistic about your routine and consider how much daily effort and time you can commit to styling your hair.

Also, keep in mind that length is a personal choice. “One thing I really dislike is when women are told they need to cut their hair after a certain age,” says Rodger Azadganian, a hairstylist, salon owner, and founder of Äz Haircare. “I think that’s such an outdated idea. I care much more about the condition of the hair and whether the cut suits the person. If you love long hair, keep it long,” he encourages. Iris completely agrees, adding that shape, health, shine, and confidence are what make your hair look youthful.

Azadganian is a huge fan of a sharp bob, which he says always looks sophisticated. “I also love collarbone-length hair with a little movement around the face. And I think long, beautiful hair on a woman over 50 can be incredibly chic,” he adds.

Iris says the styles that consistently flatter his over-50 clients are those with softness around the face, deep angles, and layers if the hair is longer. Like Azadganian, Iris also says a collarbone-length lob with long soft layers is the most versatile. “It keeps length, gives movement, and [can be] styled in various ways,” says Iris. He adds that a textured bob, worn a little undone rather than perfectly smooth, is chic at any age and makes fine hair look thicker because the ends are blunt and full.

If you have or prefer short hair, Iris says a soft pixie with a longer, swept fringe is beautiful and lifts the whole face. “Face-framing layers or a soft curtain fringe are my secret weapon—they draw the eye up, soften everything, and grow out gracefully between appointments.”

Gray hair grows in with a different texture than you may be used to. It’s often wirier, drier, and more resistant to products and color, so styles may need more moisture and smoothing than before.

For those who dye their hair all one color and have started to, or are already fully gray, regrowth may pose an unflattering problem. “Solid color against graying regrowth creates a harsh line and can actually make thinning at the part more visible,” Iris cautions. Instead, both Iris and Azadganian recommend dimensional color over anything too flat or solid. Go for softer dimension—highlights, lowlights, or a blended “gray blending” approach. This technique diffuses the regrowth, adds the illusion of depth and fullness, and buys you more time between appointments.

If you’re embracing grey fully, love it. The key, though, according to Iris, is to keep it bright and conditioned. “Grey hair can yellow and look dull; a glossing treatment and the right purple shampoo, like John Frieda Violet Crush, keep it silver and striking instead of dusty. Color should work with your styling, never make it harder,” Iris recommends.

For fine hair, both Azadganian and Iris suggest keeping the ends blunt in order to make the hair look fuller. “A bob or lob with a strong perimeter makes hair look twice as dense, and I’d avoid heavy layering, which removes the fullness you can see. Fullness also has to be built at the styling,” Iris explains. He recommends the John Frieda Ultra Filler+ shampoo and conditioner for fine hair to plump each strand and to make hair look and feel thicker when styled. Use a thickening spray at the root before blow-drying for lasting lift.

For thick or coarse hair, go with longer layers to take out some weight and keep it from looking heavy and triangular. For curly and coily hair, Iris says shape is key. “A cut done on dry hair, in its natural state, with layers that let the curl sit where it lives, plus real moisture.”

Azadganian says a beautifully shaped curly bob is incredible. “I always want the haircut to work with the texture instead of forcing it into something else,” he notes.

Wavy hair looks best in a mid-length cut with soft layers that enhance the natural bend of the texture without heat styling. As for length, Iris says short hair loves texture and a little messiness. “Mid-length, chin to collarbone, is the sweet spot for most women because it’s long enough to feel feminine and short enough to stay full,” says Iris. Long hair after 50 absolutely works, but as both stylists noted, only if the hair is kept healthy and styled.

Azadganian says one of the biggest mistakes with hair over age 50 is getting stuck with the same hairstyle for decades. “You don’t need to change everything, but even updating your layers, fringe, or part can make such a difference. I love seeing women experiment with their hair at every age,” says Azadganian.

#6700. 6 Gift-Worthy Products That Changed My Life in November

Chances are you are reading this while awash in the joyful glow of an active oven and/or open laptop chock full of Black Friday sales. As someone who can’t help but enjoy America’s annual week of consumption, I have some advice: You can probably skip that fourth slice of pie and also, when it comes to gift-giving, bigger is not always better. Personally, some of the best presents I’ve received over the years are small but meaningful items that come with a sense of personal connection and discovery: A set of irresistible Japanese incense from an aspirational boutique my fiancé found in Tokyo’s Aoyama district. Some adorable silk painted ornaments that my mom stumbled upon at a local maker’s market. Employee merch from NASA, where my stepfather worked. All that to say, the ritual of gift-giving is always made to be much more meaningful when we can collect all the little, delightful items we’ve collected over the year and share them with those whom will appreciate them most. Maybe that’s a simple, but utterly practical thingamajig that has made your life that much more convenient. Or something you came across in your travels. Even an item you have at home for years that you couldn’t imagine living without. Here are a few self-care accessories that I've encountered both recently and not-so-recently that fit that bill.

Ever since the days of chewable Flintstones vitamins, we as a human race have been striving for new, inventive ways to get our daily dose in. Straight pills are boring and require water. Powders are messy and not easily transported. Gummies can be overly sugary and harden over time. But strips are nearly weightless, easy to travel with, and consumable on their own. The ones made by StripIt dissolve on your tongue, a little like a Listerine mint strip, and come in four different formulas: Vitamin C, D3 + K2, B12, Sleep, and Digest. Though they can take a little bit longer than a mint strip to dissolve, they’re still a really convenient way to make sure you’re keeping up with your supplements. I’ve been using the D3 + K2 ones while I travel. They taste pretty good and are colorful and novel enough to function as a small stocking stuffer or Hanukkah gift for the health hawk in your life.

I will never tire of a big, beautiful block of soap. Especially if it's so fragrant that it fills a whole room with its smell. My new favorite in this category is Life Research Lab, an herb-forward brand that takes inspiration from (and incorporates) the local LA flora into its soap blends. There’s the eucalyptus and grapefruit pumice slab that draws from common neighborhood trees, a classic “cowboy cologne” mix of cypress and California sagebrush so commonly found in Angelenos’ gardens, and a juniper bergamot number that lifts scents from the desert. Many of them contain bits and pieces of the very plants they’re named for, which function as light exfoliants as you scrub. I was especially charmed by the Christmas Morning Soap, which is infused with balsam fir essential oil and pinyon pine leaf. Its name, price, and the fact that it comes in both 9-ounce blocks and 4-ounce cylinders make it a perfect stocking stuffer. T’is the season.

When I traveled to Seoul a few years ago, I popped into the Hinok store and spent about 20 minutes smelling basically every one of its products, which all contain ingredients sourced by foraging detritus from old cypress forests on Jeju island (not cutting them down). Alas, I couldn’t bring anything home because my carry-on was already stuffed. But recently I learned the brand is available to buy in the US and had a chance to try a few things out. My favorite is the thick, gooey body wash. It has the consistency of honey, and emanates a light, fresh natural scent without being too overpowering. Most crucially, the formula is gentle enough to clean your body without stripping it of its natural oils. After searching for a light body wash I can use daily for a while now, this qucikly became my new favorite. (The deodorising spray is really good for households with pets too.)

When I was in New York last month, my future sister in-law suggested we take a quick field trip to Senti Senti, a little skincare store in Chinatown. To my delight, they carried imported Korean and Japanese formulas of lotions, cleansers, and—most importantly—sunscreen from popular brands like Beauty of Joseon and Bioré. (Don’t ask me how this is allowed, Chinatown’s gonna Chinatown.) In the 15 minutes we spent there, we observed at least three people walk in and beeline for this Dr. Althea face lotion and I couldn’t help but be tempted. Using it for the past month, I’ve been impressed with how light, hydrating, and easily absorbing it is—an excellent goop to apply in the morning. For anyone who hates the lingering feeling of a heavy day cream, this one’s a no-brainer.

We’re days away from the start of winter, meaning we’ll all be heating up our homes and braving, windy, sub-zero temperatures more regularly for the next few months. All of that means our skin has a far higher chance of getting dry and flaky. Enter Bein All-Over Body Oil. The blend of organic sesame seed, squalane, and camellia oil rubs on smoothly and absorbs like a dream, leaving no sticky residue behind. Body oil can be especially helpful for feet, knees, elbows, and hands, post-shower. And is light enough that you can apply lotion on afterward, too. Best of all, the San Diego-brand’s products smell faintly of bitter citrus, like a little Aperol spritz for your skin. It’s bright-red packaging makes it feel especially festive for the holidays.

A good robe, in my humble opinion, must rise to a few crucial tasks. It should be able to absorb water well, dry out quickly, and let your skin breathe after a long warm soak in the bath or shower. It should also allow you to lounge around your household without flashing your living companions or getting furniture wet. That’s why I’m partial to a long linen kimono, which checks all of those boxes. I was gifted a sophisticated blue-striped one from Deiji Studios nearly six years ago for Christmas, and it’s now a perfectly worn-in, indispensable bathroom staple. No wonder Deiji still sells it. Even better, it’s on sale for Black Friday.

#6699. Before You Shop Glossier’s Black Friday Sale, Here’s Our Ranking of the 15 Best Products

Once the new kid on the block, Glossier is one of the biggest and most well-known (and loved) makeup and skincare brands out there. “Cloud Paint” and “Boy Brow” aren’t just product names now; they’re trends that developed whole product categories, igniting the launch of a zillion dupes. It’s iconic, to say the least.

Glossier became known for its minimalist approach, both in packaging and product formula. Every makeup product is filled with skin-loving ingredients (like hyaluronic acid and glycerin), the skincare is gentle and suitable for all skin types, and their approach to beauty is simple and fresh-faced. From the best-selling Balm Dotcom to makeup artist-favorite Stretch Concealer to the hidden-gem bodycare—choosing a favorite child is hard. That’s why I ranked and reviewed all of the best Glossier products below.

While we’re here... Glossier is running its annual Black Friday sale right now. FWIW, Glossier rarely goes on sale. The brand really only does discounts during its annual Black Friday sale, meaning you won’t see prices this low for likely another year. I know 25% off doesn’t feel all that buzzy or exciting (it’s not the same shock as all those TVs and gaming consoles you stood in line for as a kid), but it’s one of the highest I’ve ever seen Glossier offer. And if you spend more than $100, your discount shoots up to 30% off. That, my friends, is a steal in the Glossier world.

Did you too pluck your eyebrows just a little (read: a lot) when you were younger? Unforch, the sentiment is v relatable, but this brow gel helps give that fluffy appearance even when you don’t have too much going on above your eyelids. “It's the only brow gel that visibly thickens my brows upon application,” editor Corinne Sullivan notes. “My favorite thing is how it keeps my brows conditioned rather than crunchy like other products do.”

When it comes to blush, Glossier’s beginner-friendly Cloud Paint will forever remain a cult-favorite pick among beauty lovers. “Sorry, but Cloud Paint is still the GOAT of all cream blushes,” beauty editor Beth Gillette shares. “Yup, I said it!!! It blends better than any other formula I've used, especially with my fingers, making it practically the simplest blush to apply ever. The shades are timeless and trendy and I'll never get tired of 'em.”

Beauty director Lauren Balsamo is obsessed with these blushes (she even said it was her favorite makeup launch last year). “The original Cloud Paint provides a nice wash of color that lasts for an hour or two, but Plush Blush lasts all day without fading, even on my extremely oily skin,” she says. “I’m not someone who wears primer or sets my makeup with a setting spray, so I fully expect most makeup to fade after a few hours, but Plush Blush survived a 12-hour office day on my cheeks.”

If dewy glasslike skin is what you’re after, dewy glasslike is what you’ll get! As the name suggests, Futuredew is the oil-serum hybrid that will make future you dewy as hell. Packed with nourishing oils and plant-based extracts, you’ll be looking as radiant as ever in no time. Wear it alone, under makeup, or as a highlighter on the high points of your face.

Makeup artist Katie Jane Hughes got me obsessed with this brow pencil, and now it’s virtually the only one I’ll use. It has a unique, straight tip (versus the usual fine, pointy one) that helps create hairlike strokes and fill in my brows with the same tool. My brows look natural and soft every time.

Glossier might not have been the first to launch a lip oil, but it made a pretty damn good one. It makes my lips look plush and glossy and hydrates like a lip treatment. The tints are extremely sheer too, so it works with basically every lip liner I pair it with.

Where skincare and makeup come to play, this foundation feels like a smooth gel cream that melts into the skin like butter. Available in 32 shades, you can build the coverage of this foundation up or keep it as minimal as you’d like. As for its ingredients, hydration is the name of the game with a mix of squalane and glycerin.

Lip Line is one of my favorite lip liners ever, and I own more than 50 (sorry, I’m a lip liner fiend). I love how easily the formula glides onto my lips, but the best part is how long it lasts. I don’t always want a stain, but I do often want a liner I don’t have to reapply right away. These stay on for hours without needing to pull it out of my bag and re-do, and they do so without drying out my lips.

As a lipstick lover, Ultralip remains a top-tier product. The main reason is the super hydrating formula that still gives a stunning color payoff. “For long-lasting color and tons of moisture, this is my go-to balm!” editor Corinne Sullivan shares. “It provides a nice, subtle tint while keeping my lips smooth and shiny (even in the winter!).”

Looking in the mirror and seeing your under-eyes creased is a top-five woe of makeup users all around. Stretch Balm concealer is flexible, lightweight, and hydrating. It covers up all the dark circles and redness you might wake up with after a long night of watching Eras Tour livestreams (I see you, I hear you, and I am you).

Let’s be real: Not all cleansers easily take off all of your makeup in one go. This gentle solution has a pH-balanced formula that actually removes your mascara and eye makeup without excess scrubbing. It’s creamy and nourishing so you don’t have to worry about your face feeling stripped and dry after.

What can we say, we’re fans of the classics! Balm Dotcom is one of the brand’s best-selling products, and for good reason. “Let the record show that Balm Dot Calm is still one of the best lip balms out there, despite having spawned hundreds of copycats since it first took over our feeds,” says commerce writer Sarah Maberry. “Old-school Glossier fans will be happy to hear they returned to the OG formula but kept the new and improved packaging. For years, I've kept a few of the birthday cake flavor on hand for an easy last-minute gift that everyone loves. I've even had friends tell me they look forward to their annual restock.”

If you wish you could bathe in Glossier You, you’ll enjoy dousing yourself in this body butter. It’s rich and decadent as it hydrates skin with shea butter and jojoba oil. I keep the chic jar on my nightstand and coat my skin with this cream before bed, and it makes my skin soft and supple (and smell amazing).

If I could lather myself in this lotion at all times, I would. As someone with oily skin, the idea of feeling sticky/greasy after lotioning is a constant fear. But this one absorbs into the skin so well while also giving off a subtle glowing finish. It literally feels like soft silk and smells so good with its orange blossom neroli complex.

A dab of this underneath my eyes is like a primer for my concealer. It immediately plumps my skin, making my under-eyes look brighter, firmer, and more awake.

Beth Gillette is the beauty editor at Cosmopolitan with more than eight years of experience researching, writing, and editing skin, hair, makeup, nail, and fragrance stories.

Prior to Cosmopolitan, she wrote and edited beauty content as an Editor at The Everygirl for four years. Follow her on Instagram for makeup selfies and a new hair 'do every few months.

#6698. These Perfumes Are Basically Main Character Energy in a Bottle

Discover the scents that turn an everyday entrance into a scene.

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

We’re all the main characters of our own lives, so why not step into the part with a fragrance that makes you feel like one?

When studied, main characters are often going through a transformation: change (Katniss Everdeen), personal growth (Elle Woods), or hardship (Andy Sachs). Almost all of them find their inner confidence by the end of the story, at least in the more true-to-life films.

That confidence is exactly what a great fragrance can help you channel. Scent is closely tied to memory and emotion, which is why a single spritz can shift your mood before you’ve even left the house. Think of it as your personal soundtrack: a warm vanilla and amber for your cozy, golden-hour scene, a crisp bergamot and white floral for the day you walk into the room ready to take charge, a smoky, woody blend for your mysterious entrance.

The right perfume can help you feel like the person you already are, only more so. Check out some of the fragrances below that instantly make you feel and step into main character energy.

She’s warm, spicy, and impossible to miss. This Kayali fragrance is for the main character who's confident, authentic, and entirely herself, bringing warmth to everyone around her.

She's an It girl by default. Chanel's Gabrielle captures the charming essence of Gabrielle "Coco" Chanel herself, before she created the House of Chanel. This floral fragrance is rich, feminine, and sparkling with freshness. It's perfect for go-getters who treasure chic in their lives.

Key Notes: Jasmine, Ylang-Ylang, Orange Blossom, Grasse Tuberose

Cherry Stem is a fruity floral that feels sexy and mysterious beneath a sweet facade. She may seem innocent at first, but she definitely knows what she’s doing.

Key notes: Black Cherry, Red Freesia, Ebony Woods

Since its debut, Glossier You has made us all feel like main characters by settling uniquely into our skin. It’s soft, warm, and familiar, with a skin-like scent unlike any other.

Key notes: Pink Pepper, Iris, Ambrette + Ambrox

For the one who has chosen to leave shyness behind, this sweet, seductive perfume is rich and fragrant without being overpowering. It’s made for stepping into any room and owning it.

Key notes: Neroli, Orange Blossom, Honeysuckle, Marshmallow, Accord

This signature vanilla scent is for the character who knows her worth and isn't afraid to show it. She's known by her fragrance, and everyone knows when she's entered the room. Warm, creamy, and impossible to forget, it's the scent that lingers long after she's gone.

If Marie Antoinette were a fragrance, this would be it. Soft, powdery, yet bold and ultra feminine all at once, Delina is a floral, musky fragrance that delivers luxury with every whiff. It’s made for the main character who loves a little drama and never apologizes for taking up space.

Key notes: Rhubarb, Lychee, Turkish Rose, Cashmeran

Wild at heart, elegant by design. This herbal floral hair oil is a study in contrast: green, untamed notes meet soft petals for a scent that feels like a walk through a garden at dawn. It’s for the main character who refuses to blend in and wouldn't want to.

Key Notes: Galbanum, Clary Sage, Tuberose, Ylang-Ylang

Inspired by the Greek myth of Eros and Psyche, this delicate scent feels crisp, fresh, and romantic, a love story you'll never tire of wearing.

Key notes: Musk, Iris, Ambrette, Pink Peppercorn

This main character is a Brooklynite who loves jazz and can usually be found at one of the neighborhood's music clubs on the weekend, and probably on a weekday, too. This warm, spicy fragrance suits her perfectly, with a smoky depth that lingers like the last note of a late-night set.

Key Notes: Pink Pepper, Rum Absolute, Tobacco Leaf Absolute

She’s the new girl in town, and suddenly she has all the attention. Blended from the scents of Lebanon, including cedar, fig, sandalwood, amber, and musk, it's made for turning heads.

Key Notes: Cardamom, Cedarwood, Cashmere, Sandalwood, Amber

For a love story that stirs deep emotion, Closer is a toasty, creamy scent that reveals itself gradually through barley tea, toasted coconut, cashmere, and tonka bean. Created to bring you closer to that special someone, it lingers softly on the skin and leaves an impression long after you’ve said goodnight.

Key Notes: Barley Tea, Toasted Coconut, Oat, Soft Musks

This fragrance pays tribute to the iconic rose and the WWI frontline nurses who saved lives with strength, grace, and compassion. It’s for the protectors who stand strong with grace and lead with a gentle heart, proving that softness and resilience can live in the same scent.

Key Notes: Pink Pepper, Saffron, Black Raspberry, Black Amber
